Duplicate titles therefore collapse to one ID, an out-of-scope same-title row can be overbound, and deletion of the actual in-prompt row may not cancel. Temporal text and temporal ID are likewise selected in separate queries, creating a TOCTOU mismatch (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1384-1423`, `:1689-1762`). Return IDs with the exact rows used to format list/temporal metadata in one authoritative read instead of reverse-resolving presentation text; add duplicate-title, out-of-scope collision, and changed-latest regressions. **Blocker — saved-meeting Deep can publish sources whose snippets were truncated out of the final provider prompt.** The authoritative builder receives the entire persisted-context budget, then every non-duplicate Deep source is appended without any remaining-budget check (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1004-1012`, `:1098-1126`). `assemble_prompt` subsequently truncates the oversized context block but `sources` is not pruned to match (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1349-1377`, `:2545-2599`). On a baseline that fills its budget, planner-added snippets disappear from the actual prompt while their source chips remain, violating exact retained-evidence/source parity; current tests use a small fixture and only assert parity when no truncation is triggered (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:5677-5743`). Merge additions through one remaining-budget/source-retention step and add a saturated authoritative-summary/notes/R10 fixture proving mandatory anchors remain and every final source is verbatim in the actually assembled prompt. **Should-fix — source-less stream deletion still emits no abort event, so the UI can remain busy even when registry invalidation worked.** `stream_chat` binds `inputs.prompt_meeting_ids`, but its direct `chat-stream-abort` branch is selected only when source existence fails (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:2989-3005`, `:3068-3093`). For a started meeting-list/temporal stream with `sources == []`, deletion cancels/removes ownership, the empty source check succeeds, and subsequent done/error emission is ownership-suppressed without the abort needed to scrub/reset ChatPanel. The backend race test uses a source-bearing fixture and the frontend test injects an abort event, so this combined path is untested (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:7884-7985`; `frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx:271-315`). Drive abort from typed deletion invalidation/prompt-ID existence rather than source absence alone, and add a real source-less start→delete regression plus stale/replaced event assertions. The high-surrogate/non-low validation is correctly ordered before subtraction and valid-pair behavior remains covered (`frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/chat.rs:791-848`; `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/meeting.rs:575-590`, `:702-718`). Fast/MCP/live mode selection, strict one-meeting Deep allow-list, shared cancellation, MCP admission/deadline, malformed-source global bounds, and prior source-bearing deletion fences remain structurally intact, but the defects above prevent R63 acceptance. Independent verification passes Rust lib 819 / 0 / 2, `cargo check` with exactly the documented pre-existing `Sidebar` warning, typecheck, Vitest 102 / 102 across 20 files with existing stderr noise, and touched-file rustfmt; scoped `git diff --check` exits successfully with a CRLF→LF warning for `frontend/src/types/index.ts`. ### Review R65 — after Task HR-4.5.R64
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-terra
- Findings (ordered by impact):
  1. **Blocker — truncated meeting lists retain an unbound aggregate for meetings whose rows were not retained.** The list heading always renders `meetings.len()` before title lines are budgeted, while the ID set receives only IDs for lines that fit (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1766-1789`). Thus a tight context can retain “(100 total)” and zero/few titles, but `prompt_meeting_ids` excludes the omitted 100/few rows when it is later fenced (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1001-1011`, `:1434-1440`). Deleting one omitted row after preparation neither invalidates the request nor fails any exact-ID check, allowing the provider to answer with stale meeting-count metadata. This contradicts final-prompt-only identity transport and the no-overbinding rule; the duplicate-title test uses a 64k budget and cannot reveal it (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:5024-5045`). Make the heading describe only the fully retained title lines (or carry and validate every contributor to any rendered aggregate), and add a tiny-budget deletion race including an omitted duplicate/out-of-scope row. The analogous today-count metadata is also retained without a member ID (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:977-987`); either establish that aggregate counts are deliberately non-sensitive or apply the same final-retention rule.
  2. **Blocker — deletion abort delivery is not atomic with stream start or terminal publication, so a current source-less stream can remain busy with partial deleted-context output.** After the post-bind check succeeds, a deletion can set the retained invalidation marker before the independently fenced start emit; that emit then returns false and `stream_chat` returns `Ok(())` without publishing abort (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:3066-3088`, `:3299-3317`). The same gap exists after the terminal existence check and marker poll but before the subsequent done/error ownership publication (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:3132-3204`): cancellation suppresses done/error, yet no abort is sent. ChatPanel can only scrub/reset upon the abort event (`frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx:406-424`), so it remains loading/streaming or shows partial content in these interleavings. Existing backend tests delete while generation is blocked and therefore before the terminal poll; the frontend test injects the already-delivered event (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:8182-8251`; `frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx:318-342`). Decide abort-vs-start/done atomically under the registry lock, and add deterministic races for check→start and terminal-poll→done that assert exactly one safe abort, no terminal event/persisted answer, zero registrations, and a restored panel.

The two outer 300-second stream timeouts now resolve ownership, deletion invalidation, cancellation, abort emission, and registry removal in one `ChatRequestState` lock transition. The non-publication terminal paths after stream start use that same transition, so a retained deletion marker cannot be cleared without its safe abort.
- Files changed: `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs` (atomic timeout/terminal cleanup transition and deterministic timeout interleaving), `frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` (active timeout-race abort cleanup before command completion), this doc.

The UI regression leaves command completion pending until after abort and proves the active row/sources are scrubbed, sending is restored, and no assistant message is saved.

Treat an indeterminate terminal fence as a content-free, stable-reason abort (distinct from confirmed deletion if needed), atomically clear ownership, and add a real started-stream database-failure regression that proves no row/source remains and the panel is usable.

The R65-R68 atomic terminal transitions were assessed as correct: ownership, deletion precedence, cancellation, event choice, and registry removal resolve under one lock, so a deletion-invalidated stream emits exactly one identity-only abort, a started stream whose terminal revalidation fails or times out emits exactly one source-free `safeCleanup` error, and a replaced or unstarted request emits neither.
- Findings (severity order):
  1. **High — a planner `openMeetingIds` action is a no-op whenever fusion already ranked hydration's meeting cap.** `append_authoritative_evidence` appends the opened meeting at `ranking.meetings.len() + 1` (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs`), but `hydrate` publishes only the first `MAX_HYDRATED_MEETINGS = 5` ranked meetings carrying citable evidence (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/hydration.rs`). In `All`/`Folder` scope — the scopes Deep ships for — fusion routinely ranks that many, so the open costs a database load and a planner round and then reaches neither context nor sources. Task 4.2's "a fixture requiring a second meeting open succeeds without scope widening" holds only below that cap.
  2. **Medium — `expandEvidenceIds` bypasses the planner open budget.** `expand_neighborhood_targets` yields an empty `segment_ids` vector for summary/notes evidence (no transcript endpoints), and the expand loop then performed an empty-target load, which selects the whole meeting and publishes its head — an implicit open counted against neither `PLANNER_MAX_OPENS_PER_ROUND` (5) nor `PLANNER_MAX_OPENS_TOTAL` (8). Ten summary evidence IDs from ten distinct meetings per round reach twenty effectively-opened meetings per request. Scope was never widened (every ID is retained in-scope evidence), but the enforced limit was not the recorded architecture limit.
  3. **Medium — a started stream could end with no terminal event.** `stream_chat` classified generation failures by substring (`e.to_lowercase().contains("cancelled")`) and that arm publishes nothing unless a deletion marker is set. `generate_summary_stream` embeds the raw provider body verbatim, so an upstream body containing "cancelled" took the arm although the token was alive and no deletion occurred; the registration was removed, `stream_chat` returned `Ok(())`, and ChatPanel — which had already rendered an assistant row with source chips from `chat-stream-start` — never received done, error, or abort. This defeats the "exactly one terminal signal per started stream" invariant R65-R68 established.
  4. **Medium — planner opens loaded the entire meeting transcript to publish eight segments.** The empty-target load is capped at `MAX_TRANSCRIPT_ROWS = 10_000`, while `AUTHORITATIVE_SEGMENTS_PER_MEETING = 8` is all that is published; with up to eight opens per Deep turn that is up to 80,000 discarded rows inside the 30-second budget, and the module doc's "bounded transcript head" claim did not match the code.
  5. **Medium — Deep progress copy reported completion at stage start.** The agent emits `InitialRetrieval { completed: 0, total: 0 }` before initial retrieval, and the panel rendered "Initial retrieval complete: 0 of 0." for the whole of the longest silent phase — the "silent preparation reads as a hang" failure stage-level progress exists to prevent.
  6. **Low — raw database error text still reached the UI on the pre-start paths.** `ensure_prompt_meetings_exist` formatted the SQLite error into its message, which propagates through the command-rejection channel from preparation, the pre-start stream fence, and the non-streaming/MCP fences, and ChatPanel renders it verbatim — inconsistent with the sanitation contract R67/R68 established for post-start signals.
  7. **Low — MCP chat never reported its own timeout.** The deadline path passed `Ok(Err("Chat request timed out"))` into `finish_non_streaming_chat_request`, which sees `timed_out == false` on the outer `Ok`, then observes the cancelled token and returns "Chat request was cancelled"; a client cannot tell a deadline from deletion invalidation.
  8. **Low — `deep_preparation_eligible` carried a dead parameter and a stale contract comment** (`_today_meeting_ids` ignored at both call sites; the comment still claimed saved-meeting paths stay separate although R63 added a saved-meeting Deep branch above it).
  9. **Low — the blanket context truncation could break parity silently.** `truncate_at_char_boundary(&context, persisted_context_budget)` ran for every scope without reconciling `sources`; it is a no-op today, which is exactly why a future builder that overshot would publish sources whose text was cut from the prompt without failing.
  10. **Low — the planner truncation notice under-reported what was dropped.** `offerable` was incremented inside the loop that breaks at the first block that does not fit, so the notice always read "N of N+1 items shown" regardless of how much ranked evidence the cap excluded — and the planner uses that notice to choose finish vs search_more.
- Residual risks: Deep p50/p95, provider round-trip cost, and the Fast/Deep quality delta remain unmeasured, so the recorded Deep-as-default decision cannot be revisited at sprint close; up-to-100 authoritative broad loads remain sequential and untimed; `hydrate_broad_scope` issues roughly three sequential round trips per member; repository-wide `cargo fmt --check` still fails on the rejected V10 fixture whitespace; and all R63-R68 work remains uncommitted, so none of it is covered by the reviewed commit range.
- Inherited Sprint 3 release gates (unchanged, each still absent): valid independently authored Portuguese corpus; production-path quality and final provider-answer evidence; native Windows/R13 hermetic session evidence; exact-head GitHub Actions evidence. No rejected corpus or corpus-free diagnostic was accepted as evidence.
- Follow-up tasks created: HR-4.5.R70

### Task HR-4.5.R70 — R70 remediation: planner open/expand authority and cost, terminal stream signal, sanitised fences [M]

- Date: 2026-09-03
- Implementer model: anthropic/claude-opus-5
- Status: done (implementation with focused tests on `sprint-2/durable-local-index`, baseline `9098a8b` plus the uncommitted R63-R68 tree; Task HR-4.5 remains **Blocked** on its four inherited Sprint 3 gates. No release claim, no Deep-as-default change, no commit.)
- Scope: close all ten R70 findings. No corpus, evaluation, debug, or release artifact was read, edited, staged, or run.
- Files changed: `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/database/repositories/retrieval.rs`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs`, `frontend/src-tauri/src/mcp/server.rs`, `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx`, `frontend/src/lib/strings/en.ts`, `frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx`, this doc, `docs/hybrid-rag/sprint-4-deep-saved-scopes.md` (review record).
- Implementation:
  1. **Planner opens reach the final context (finding 1).** New `reserve_planner_hydration_slots` runs after `append_authoritative_evidence`: at most `PLANNER_HYDRATED_MEETING_RESERVE = 2` meetings that fusion ranked OUTSIDE `MAX_HYDRATED_MEETINGS` are promoted to the last slots inside it, every other meeting keeps fusion order, and ranks are renumbered to stay a dense 1-based ordering. The planner can therefore never displace the top fusion results, and hydration is never widened past its own cap. `MAX_HYDRATED_MEETINGS` is imported from hydration rather than duplicated.
  2. **Range-free expansions are charged against the open budget (finding 2).** An expansion whose evidence carries no transcript endpoints is an open in everything but name, so the expand loop now checks `PLANNER_MAX_OPENS_TOTAL` and inserts into `opened_meetings` before loading; a meeting already opened this request is skipped instead of re-loaded. The enforced limit now equals the recorded architecture limit.
  3. **Bounded head loads (finding 4).** New `RetrievalRepository::load_meeting_source_head_with_cancellation(pool, meeting_id, head_segments, cancel)` selects the first `head_segments` rows in canonical order with `LIMIT ?` and delegates to the existing range loader; `load_meeting_source_compact_with_cancellation` is now that function with `head_segments = 1`, so broad hydration is unchanged. Planner opens and range-free expansions call it with `AUTHORITATIVE_SEGMENTS_PER_MEETING`, replacing the empty-target whole-meeting select.
  4. **Cancellation is decided by the token, not the provider's text (finding 3).** `stream_chat`'s cancellation arm is now guarded by `token.is_cancelled()`. Anything the token does not confirm as cancelled falls through to the error arm, which always publishes a terminal event, so a started stream can no longer be stranded with a rendered source-bearing row. Deletion precedence is untouched: the marker is still consumed first by `finish_chat_stream_if_current`.
  5. **Sanitised existence fence (finding 6).** `ensure_prompt_meetings_exist` logs the raw SQLite error and returns the same stable, content-free `CHAT_STREAM_REVALIDATION_ERROR` the post-start terminal paths already publish, so no database internals reach the command-rejection channel or the panel. The deleted-meeting condition keeps its own distinct typed error.
  6. **MCP reports its own deadline (finding 7).** The deadline branch records `timed_out` and, after the ownership gate runs, restores `MCP_CHAT_TIMEOUT_ERROR` as the surfaced cause instead of the generic cancellation the token now carries. Ownership release, guard idempotence, and the no-generation guarantee are unchanged.
  7. **Dead parameter removed (finding 8).** `deep_preparation_eligible(retrieval_mode)` drops `_today_meeting_ids`, and its doc now states the real rule: forced-lexical, an unavailable lifecycle, and live scope downgrade to Fast before this is consulted, and MCP rejects Deep before preparation.
  8. **Parity-preserving budget backstop (finding 9).** The blanket truncation became a `debug_assert!` plus a release backstop that, if a builder ever overshoots, truncates AND drops every source whose snippet no longer appears in the context, with a warning log. A published source can no longer reference text the model did not receive.
  9. **Honest planner truncation notice (finding 10).** `offerable` is counted over the whole ranked evidence list before the emission loop, so the notice reports the real omission the planner is deciding against.
  10. **Deep progress copy (finding 5).** New `chat.preparation.initialRetrievalStarted` ("Searching your meetings for relevant evidence...") renders for the entry event (`total == 0`); only the second announcement, carrying the retained counts, claims completion.
- Focused regressions added:
  - `retrieval::agent::tests::a_planner_open_reaches_the_final_context_past_the_hydration_meeting_cap` — seven matching meetings; phase one learns the real fusion order and asserts the target starts OUTSIDE the published set; phase two opens exactly that meeting and proves it reaches the final context while publication stays within `MAX_HYDRATED_MEETINGS`.
  - `retrieval::agent::tests::a_planner_open_loads_only_the_bounded_transcript_head` — a 41-segment meeting carded by a weak match; an open retains at most `AUTHORITATIVE_SEGMENTS_PER_MEETING + 3` of its segments while its authoritative total stays 41.
  - `api::chat::tests::provider_error_naming_cancellation_still_terminates_a_started_stream` — a one-shot 502 whose body says the upstream request was cancelled; the request token stays alive, and the events are exactly `chat-stream-start` then `chat-stream-error` with the registry drained.
  - `api::chat::tests::mcp_timeout_cancellation_reaches_gated_preparation_and_stops_before_generation` — assertion corrected from the previous (wrong) "Chat request was cancelled" to `MCP_CHAT_TIMEOUT_ERROR`; the gated-preparation cancellation, no-generation, and capacity-reclaim proofs are unchanged.
  - `tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` progress case — the 0/0 entry event must render "Searching your meetings" and must NOT claim completion; the counted announcement still renders "2 of 3".
- Verification (repo root; `$env:CARGO_TARGET_DIR = Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA "meetily-cargo-target"`):
  - `cargo test --manifest-path "frontend/src-tauri/Cargo.toml" --lib` — ✓ 834 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored.
  - `cargo check --manifest-path "frontend/src-tauri/Cargo.toml"` — ✓ with only the documented pre-existing `variant Sidebar is never constructed` dead-code warning.
  - `pnpm --dir "frontend" run typecheck` — ✓. `pnpm --dir "frontend" exec vitest run` — ✓ 105 passed / 20 files (existing React `act(...)` stderr).
  - Touched-file `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check` on `api/chat.rs`, `mcp/server.rs`, `retrieval/agent.rs`, `retrieval/agent/tests.rs`, `database/repositories/retrieval.rs` — ✓ clean. Repository-wide `cargo fmt --check` remains blocked solely by the pre-existing rejected V10 fixture whitespace (not inspected, not modified).
  - `git diff --check` on the touched source trees — ✓ (only the pre-existing CRLF→LF notice for `frontend/src/types/index.ts`).
  - `cargo test --test retrieval_evaluation` — intentionally NOT run: the harness and corpus fixtures are excluded and cannot be evidence.
- Notes/decisions:
  1. **Why a reservation rather than an unbounded promotion.** `openMeetingIds` is already restricted to the cards the round's prompt emitted, so an opened meeting always has some ranked evidence; the defect was purely that hydration's cap is applied to fusion order while planner intent is appended behind it. Reserving two of five slots keeps the top three fusion meetings untouchable, so a compromised or confused planner cannot evict the best results, and hydration's own per-request cap is unchanged.
  2. **Why range-free expansions are charged, not refused.** Refusing them outright would silently drop a legitimate planner request; charging them keeps the behaviour useful while making the enforced budget equal the documented one. A meeting already opened this request is skipped rather than re-loaded, so the accumulator cannot be inflated by repeating the same target.
  3. **Why the token decides cancellation.** The provider's message text is untrusted for control flow: it is a remote body embedded verbatim. Deletion invalidation and user/timeout cancellation both cancel the request token, so the token is the complete and authoritative signal; everything else is a provider failure that must terminate the stream.
  4. **Release posture unchanged.** No gate, threshold, default, or release claim was altered. Task HR-4.5 remains Blocked on the four inherited Sprint 3 gates, and no measurement of Deep latency, round-trips, or quality delta was produced or implied here.

### Task HR-4.5.R72 — R72 remediation: committed-deletion notification scrubs loaded chat sources [M]
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Implementer model: z-ai/glm-5.3-flash
- Status: done (implementation with focused tests on branch `sprint-2/durable-local-index`, HEAD `093ff38`; Task HR-4.5 remains **Blocked** on its four inherited Sprint 3 gates. No release claim, no Deep-as-default change, no commit.)
- Scope: Mechanical R72 correction only — close exactly the loaded-completed-message source gap without touching rejected corpus/evaluation/debug artifacts, prior records, Sprint 5, or any release claim. (1) After a successful COMMITTED meeting deletion, `api_delete_meeting` emits one local `chat-meeting-deleted` Tauri event through the established event mechanism whose payload carries only the deleted meeting ID; nothing is emitted before commit, on rollback, or on a missing-meeting no-op, and no content/snippet/source data is ever carried. (2) ChatPanel registers one panel-level listener for that event and immediately removes the deleted meeting's source entries/navigation metadata from EVERY loaded message (history, completed, and active rows) while retaining answer/user text, other meetings' sources, and the orphan-thread disclosure — no reload, no re-persistence, safe on missing/malformed source arrays, idempotent for stale/duplicate events. (3) The active-stream deletion abort/cleanup protocol, request ownership, sidebar behavior, cancellation, and source persistence are unchanged; no second registry or event bus exists.
- Event ordering (exact): `MeetingsRepository::delete_meeting` runs its `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (invalidation hook inside the transaction, pre-commit) → `transaction.commit()` → the repository returns `Ok(true)` → `emit_chat_meeting_deleted_if_committed` (api/chat.rs) matches `Ok(true)` and emits `chat-meeting-deleted` with `{ "meetingId": <id> }` via the app handle before the command returns; `Ok(false)` and `Err` emit nothing. Because the helper consumes the completed deletion result, emission is structurally after commit; the Rust regression additionally proves the meeting row is already gone when the event publishes.
- Files changed: `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs` (`CHAT_MEETING_DELETED_EVENT`, `emit_chat_meeting_deleted_if_committed`, ordering regression), `frontend/src-tauri/src/api/api.rs` (`Emitter` import; `api_delete_meeting` emits the notification after the committed deletion; unchanged stale bookkeeping), `frontend/src/types/index.ts` (`ChatMeetingDeletedPayload`), `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx` (panel-level deletion listener + per-message source scrub), `frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` (scrub regression; the delayed-stale-listener harness now targets the first `chat-stream-start` registration explicitly, since the new panel-level listener registers earlier — the test's intent and assertions are unchanged), this doc.
- Verification (repo root; `$env:CARGO_TARGET_DIR = "C:\Users\arman\cargo-target"`):
  - `cargo test --manifest-path "frontend/src-tauri/Cargo.toml" --lib` — ✓ 835 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored (was 834: net +1).
  - Focused regression — ✓ `committed_deletion_notification_is_identity_only_and_failures_emit_none` (real transaction commit emits exactly one identity-only event after the row is gone; `Ok(false)` and a rollback-shaped `Err` emit nothing).
  - `cargo check --manifest-path "src-tauri/Cargo.toml"` — ✓ with only the documented pre-existing `Sidebar` dead-code warning.
  - `pnpm run typecheck` — ✓ (`tsc --noEmit` clean).
  - `npx vitest run` — ✓ 106 passed / 20 files (was 105: net +1).
  - Focused UI regression — ✓ `npx vitest run tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` — 26 passed (completed loaded message loses only the deleted meeting's source immediately; malformed-history sources and a missing-identity event change nothing; answer text, other sources, and the orphan text survive; no re-persistence; duplicate event idempotent).
  - Touched Rust `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check` — ✓ clean (`api/chat.rs`, `api/api.rs`); `git diff --check` — ✓ (only the pre-existing CRLF→LF notice for `frontend/src/types/index.ts`). The excluded evaluation suite was intentionally NOT run.
- Notes/decisions:
  1. **Single shared deletion path.** `api_delete_meeting` is the only production caller of `MeetingsRepository::delete_meeting` (`delete_meeting_with_transaction` is private to the repository), so the notification covers every UI deletion without new plumbing. The existing in-transaction `invalidate_meeting` hook and the R63+ abort protocol are untouched; the abort remains the active-stream terminal event, while this notification is the renderer-side scrub for already-loaded state. The two compose: whichever arrives first wins, and the other is a no-op.
  2. **Payload minimality and idempotence.** The payload is `{ "meetingId": string }` only — the established stable identity — so no snippet/source/content can leak through it. The ChatPanel scrub filters `source.meetingId === meetingId` across all loaded messages with an `Array.isArray` guard (malformed loaded `sources_json` shapes and missing identities are safe no-ops), never persists, never clears streaming/busy state, and steals no focus; the orphan-thread disclosure and error rows are untouched. Repeated or stale events are naturally idempotent filters.
  3. **Listener lifecycle.** The listener is registered once per panel mount (independent of the per-send `unlistenersRef` lifecycle) with the codebase's `await listen(...)` pattern and a cancelled-registration guard, so scope switches, stream replacements, and panel unmount neither leak nor lose it. The mock-listen harness in one pre-existing test captured "first registration" implicitly; it now targets the stream-start registration by name — behavior asserted is unchanged.

Deep latency/round-trip/quality metrics and native smoke remain unmeasured/unrun; nothing unrun is reported as passed.

### Review R73 — after Task HR-4.5.R72
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: changes-requested
- Findings: **Blocker — the new renderer scrub is not ordered against conversation loading/listener registration, so a committed deletion can still leave a deleted source loaded.** `loadConversation` starts independently before the deletion-listener effect and later replaces the whole message array with its captured rows (`frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx:128-189`), while the event callback only filters the state that exists at callback time and retains no deletion identity for later loads (`frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx:219-247`). If `api_chat_get_messages` reads before deletion but its invoke response settles after the post-commit event, the callback scrubs the old/empty state and the later `setMessages(rows)` restores the stale source. If deletion commits while asynchronous `listen(...)` registration is pending, the event can be missed entirely; after unmount, the callback also lacks a `cancelled` guard during the interval before the delayed registration resolves (`frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx:222-250`). The new test cannot expose either race because its default mock installs the callback synchronously (`frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx:53-62`) and it fires deletion only after a stream-created completed row is already rendered (`frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx:381-443`); the delayed-listener regression explicitly exercises only `chat-stream-start`, not the new listener (`frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx:638-675`). Establish listener readiness before loading, retain/reapply observed deletion identities to any later loaded rows, guard stale callbacks after cleanup, and add deterministic deferred-listener/deferred-load tests. ### Task HR-4.5.R73 — R73 remediation: deletion-event/load race ordering in ChatPanel [M]
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Implementer model: z-ai/glm-5.3-flash
- Status: done (implementation with focused tests on branch `sprint-2/durable-local-index`, HEAD `093ff38` worktree; Task HR-4.5 remains **Blocked** on its four inherited Sprint 3 gates. No release claim, no Deep-as-default change, no commit.)
- Scope: Mechanical R73 correction only — close exactly the three renderer-side race findings (backend emission ordering was verified correct in review and is untouched): (1) the panel-lifetime deletion subscription is registered before the conversation-load effect, and no load result is applied until the registration resolves (`await deletionSubscriptionRef.current` at the top of `loadConversation`), so a deletion committing after the load query started can never be missed relative to that load; (2) a panel-lifetime `locallyDeletedMeetingIdsRef` set (ids only, bounded by the panel instance lifetime) records every observed deletion synchronously and idempotently, and EVERY incoming/loaded/updated source array is filtered against it before state application — loaded history rows, `chat-stream-start` sources, and `chat-stream-done` sources (the filtered set is also what `api_chat_save_message` persists, so a stale done cannot re-persist a locally deleted meeting's source) — while answer/user text, other meetings' sources, and the orphan disclosure are preserved and nothing is refetched; (3) the deletion callback checks the subscription's `cancelled` flag first, so deliveries in the window before a delayed registration resolves — or after unmount — are no-ops. No second event bus or registry exists; the active-stream abort protocol, ownership, cancellation, sidebar behavior, and server-side scrubbing are unchanged.
- Files changed: `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx` (deletion subscription effect moved before the scope effect with registration promise, cancelled-guarded callback, recorded-id set, `sanitizeSources` filter applied at load/start/done), `frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` (four deterministic race regressions), this doc.
- Lifecycle ordering (exact): panel mount issues `listen("chat-meeting-deleted", ...)` (registration promise stored in `deletionSubscriptionRef`) → the scope effect's `loadConversation` first awaits that promise → only then invokes `api_chat_get_or_create_scoped_conversation` → `api_chat_get_messages` → applies rows filtered through `sanitizeSources`. A committed deletion at any point is either already scrubbed server-side (before the SELECT) or delivered to the active listener, whose callback synchronously adds the id and scrubs current state; the later load application filters it out. Unmount sets `cancelled`, unlistens, and nulls the subscription promise; late callback deliveries do nothing.
- Tests (all deterministic, no sleeps): `applies a loaded conversation only after the deletion subscription is registered` (deferred listener registration blocks the load — the conversation invoke is asserted not to run while registration is pending); `filters a locally deleted meeting's sources from a load result that resolves after the deletion` (deletion event recorded mid-flight, deferred `api_chat_get_messages` result applied filtered); `ignores deletion events delivered after the panel unmounts` (stale callback after unmount is a no-op and a fresh panel instance inherits nothing); `scrubs the deleted meeting from active and completed rows idempotently` (one deletion scrubs an active streaming row and a completed row, keeps answer text, the other source, and the live stream; duplicate event idempotent). **Race proof:** with the R73 gate and load filter temporarily reverted to the R72-equivalent load path, the first two regressions FAIL (`invoke` runs while registration is pending; unfiltered deleted sources render); with R73 restored all pass.
- Verification (repo root; `$env:CARGO_TARGET_DIR = "C:\Users\arman\cargo-target"`):
  - `cargo test --manifest-path "frontend/src-tauri/Cargo.toml" --lib` — ✓ 835 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored (no Rust change this round).
  - `cargo check --manifest-path "src-tauri/Cargo.toml"` — ✓ with only the documented pre-existing `Sidebar` dead-code warning.
  - `pnpm run typecheck` — ✓ (`tsc --noEmit` clean).
  - `npx vitest run` — ✓ 110 passed / 20 files (was 106: net +4); focused `npx vitest run tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` — ✓ 30 passed.
  - `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check` on the touched Rust files — ✓ clean (untouched this round, re-verified); `git diff --check` — ✓ (only the pre-existing CRLF→LF notice for `frontend/src/types/index.ts`). The excluded evaluation suite was intentionally NOT run.
- Notes/decisions:
  1. **Gate placement.** The await sits before the FIRST load invoke, not merely before `setMessages`: combined with listener-before-load effect ordering, any deletion is either committed before the load's SELECT (rows already scrubbed server-side) or captured by the active listener and filtered at application. This is what closes the "event while `listen()` is pending" miss window: the load cannot resolve into state until the listener exists.
  2. **Bounded set.** `locallyDeletedMeetingIdsRef` holds only stable meeting ids observed during this panel instance's lifetime (Set.add is idempotent; no content, no persistence, no growth across panel instances). Server-side transactional scrubbing remains the authoritative persistence boundary; the client filter is the renderer-memory complement.
  3. **Done-path parity.** Filtering once in the done handler and using the filtered array for both `saveMessage` and `setMessages` keeps display and persistence consistent; for meetings deleted server-side the delayed-save sanitizer still applies, so behavior for already-scrubbed payloads is unchanged.
  4. Process note: an intermediate PowerShell rewrite of `ChatPanel/index.tsx` transiently added a UTF-8 BOM and two CRLF pairs; both were removed (file restored to BOM-less LF), confirmed by byte inspection, and the focused suite was rerun green afterwards.

### Task HR-4.5.R74 — R74 remediation: fail-closed deletion subscription, bounded epoch-tagged deleted-ID retention [M]
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Implementer model: z-ai/glm-5.3-flash
- Status: done (implementation with focused tests on branch `sprint-2/durable-local-index`, HEAD `093ff38` worktree; Task HR-4.5 remains **Blocked** on its four inherited Sprint 3 gates. No release claim, no Deep-as-default change, no commit.)
- Scope: Mechanical R74 correction only, frontend-only (the backend emission ordering was re-confirmed correct in review). (1) **Fail-closed subscription:** `deletionSubscriptionActiveRef` becomes true ONLY when `listen("chat-meeting-deleted", ...)` actually registers; while unconfirmed — or forever if registration rejects — `sanitizeSources` suppresses ALL source/navigation data from every load/start/done application, so an unobservable deletion can never leave stale sources rendering. The raw listener error is only logged, never surfaced; answer/user text, sending, and the stream lifecycle keep working, so the UI stays usable with source-free rendering (reviewer-sanctioned option "strip all source/navigation data until subscription succeeds"; no retry loop). (2) **Bounded retention:** the deleted-ID set is replaced by `locallyDeletedMeetingsRef: Map<meetingId, generation>` capped at `MAX_LOCALLY_DELETED_MEETING_IDS = 64`. Recording tags each id with the current scope generation (the panel's existing `scopeGenerationRef` epoch — no new registry); on overflow the oldest-generation entry is evicted and the epoch is marked evicted, which suppresses ALL source rendering for the remainder of that generation — invalidating every response that could carry an evicted id (a naive capped set would have re-rendered it). On scope change the epoch is bounded: prior-generation entries are purged (their responses are already blocked by the existing generation/`isCurrentRequest` guards) and the eviction degradation lifts, so non-deleted sources render again. No parallel event bus or registry exists; server-side transactional scrubbing remains authoritative; active-stream abort protocol, ownership, cancellation, sidebar behavior, and source parity are unchanged, and R73's successful-subscription ordering/filtering behavior is preserved.
- Files changed: `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx` (subscription active flag + fail-closed suppression, generation-tagged bounded map, eviction-in-epoch marker, epoch purge at scope change, `sanitizeSources` guards, cap constant), `frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` (two deterministic regressions), this doc.
- Fail-closed semantics (exact): registration rejected → `deletionSubscriptionActiveRef` stays false → every `sanitizeSources` call returns `undefined` (no source chips, no navigation metadata) for the panel lifetime; the load gate still settles (the rejected registration is caught and logged), so the panel renders text and remains fully usable; the listener callback is never registered, proving no deletion event could have been observed. Raw error text appears only in the logger, never in the DOM.
- Bound semantics (exact): ≤ 64 generation-tagged ids retained at once; oldest-generation entry evicted first on overflow; ANY eviction sets `deletedSourcesEvictedInRef = currentGeneration`, which force-suppresses all source rendering while that generation is current (so no in-flight response of the epoch can reintroduce an evicted id); a scope change purges entries older than the new generation and lifts the degradation. Memory is bounded by the cap; staleness is bounded by the epoch.
- Tests (deterministic, no sleeps): `degrades to source-free rendering when the deletion listener registration fails` (rejected listener + source-bearing deferred load + a full send/stream cycle: answer text renders, zero source chips, raw error absent from the DOM, stream completes, textarea enabled); `bounds locally retained deletions and degrades source rendering on overflow` (65 distinct deletion events saturate the 64-cap → a completed message carrying an evicted id AND a never-recorded id renders NO chips while its answer text renders; after a scope change the next generation renders a non-deleted source again and still never shows the evicted one). **Fail proof:** with the two `sanitizeSources` guards temporarily removed (R73-equivalent behavior), BOTH regressions fail (sources render with a failed listener; the evicted id's source is restored after saturation); with the guards restored all pass.
- Verification (repo root; `$env:CARGO_TARGET_DIR = "C:\Users\arman\cargo-target"`):
  - `cargo test --manifest-path "frontend/src-tauri/Cargo.toml" --lib` — ✓ 835 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored (no Rust change this round).
  - `cargo check --manifest-path "src-tauri/Cargo.toml"` — ✓ with only the documented pre-existing `Sidebar` dead-code warning.
  - `pnpm run typecheck` — ✓ (`tsc --noEmit` clean).
  - `npx vitest run` — ✓ 112 passed / 20 files (was 110: net +2); focused `npx vitest run tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` — ✓ 32 passed.
  - `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check` on the touched Rust files — ✓ clean (untouched this round, re-verified); `git diff --check` — ✓ (only the pre-existing CRLF→LF notice for `frontend/src/types/index.ts`). The excluded evaluation suite was intentionally NOT run.
- Notes/decisions:
  1. **Strip-until-succeeds over error banner.** A failed `listen()` registration degrades to source-free rendering instead of a new localized error banner: text/streams keep working, nothing raw surfaces, and the suppression is the same mechanism the eviction policy uses — one fail-closed behavior instead of two. The registration promise is caught (never a floating rejection) and the load gate settles, so no deadlock.
  2. **Why the cap alone is insufficient — and how eviction invalidates.** The only responses that can reintroduce a deleted source are ones derived from pre-deletion database state: the current generation's load and the active stream's start/done events. Eviction during a generation means a recorded id that those in-flight responses may carry is no longer known, so trust cannot be re-established for that epoch — hence whole-source suppression until the generation changes, after which every older response is blocked by the pre-existing generation/`isCurrentRequest` guards and the recorded set is purged. Bounded memory (cap) and no stale restoration (epoch invalidation) are therefore both structural.
  3. **No new infrastructure.** Reuses the panel's existing `scopeGenerationRef` epoch for tagging/purging; the "subscription active" flag is a plain ref. No bus, no second registry, no persistence, no refetch.
  4. Process note: an intermediate PowerShell edit transiently re-added a UTF-8 BOM to `ChatPanel/index.tsx`; it was stripped (byte-verified BOM-less LF) and the suite rerun green.
- Inherited Sprint 3 release gates (unchanged, each still absent): valid independently authored Portuguese corpus; production-path quality and final provider-answer evidence; native Windows/R13 hermetic session evidence; exact-head GitHub Actions evidence. Deep latency/round-trip/quality metrics and native smoke remain unmeasured/unrun; nothing unrun is reported as passed.
- Spillover: none. No rejected corpus/evaluation/debug file was read, edited, staged, or run; no commit was made.

### Review R74 — after Task HR-4.5.R73
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Reviewer model: openai/gpt-5.6-sol
- Verdict: changes-requested
- Findings:
  1. **Blocker — listener-registration failure is treated as readiness, reopening the stale-source path.** The registration promise catches and consumes `listen(...)` rejection (`frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx:157-180`), so `await deletionSubscriptionRef.current` succeeds and conversation loading proceeds (`frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx:206-230`) even though no deletion listener exists. A later committed deletion can therefore leave already-loaded sources visible, or a pre-delete SELECT can settle after deletion and restore stale sources, with no event available to record/filter the ID. The new pending-registration test covers only delayed success and has no rejection case (`frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx:445-466`). Fail closed for source metadata when registration cannot be established (or retry without allowing source-bearing state), and add a deterministic rejected-listener regression.
  2. **Should-fix — the deleted-ID set is lifetime-scoped, not size-bounded.** Every valid deletion event permanently adds an ID to `locallyDeletedMeetingIdsRef` until panel unmount (`frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx:77-86`, `:161-179`). A panel can remain mounted for an unbounded application session while meetings are repeatedly created/deleted, so the implementation's “bounded by panel lifetime” claim is not an actual memory bound. Retain only IDs needed by loaded messages or pending load/stream writers, or enforce a correctness-preserving explicit cap/lifecycle reclamation and test it.
- Tests assessed: `cargo test --manifest-path "frontend/src-tauri/Cargo.toml" --lib` ✅ 835 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored; `cargo check --manifest-path "frontend/src-tauri/Cargo.toml"` ✅ with only the existing `Sidebar` dead-code warning; `pnpm --dir "frontend" run typecheck` ✅; full Vitest ✅ 110 passed / 20 files, including 30 `chat-scope` tests, with existing React `act(...)` stderr; scoped `git diff --check` ✅ with only the existing `types/index.ts` line-ending warning. The deterministic delayed-registration and delayed-load tests do control their promises and would fail on R72; the callback is cancelled/unregistered safely on normal and delayed-success unmount paths. Excluded evaluation/corpus/debug artifacts were not inspected or run.
- Residual risks: For successful listener registration, R73 correctly orders subscription before load, records IDs before state updates, and filters exact IDs across loaded rows, stream start, stream done, UI state, and done-path persistence while preserving answer text, unrelated sources, fresh-panel isolation, orphan disclosure, active-stream state, and server-side transactional scrub. R53-R72 ownership/publication/deletion contracts remain unchanged and green. ### Task HR-4.5.R75 — R75 remediation: pending listener never blocks the panel, trust-captured source gating, mojibake cleanup [M]
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Implementer model: z-ai/glm-5.3-flash
- Status: done (implementation with focused tests on branch `sprint-2/durable-local-index`, HEAD `093ff38` worktree; Task HR-4.5 remains **Blocked** on its four inherited Sprint 3 gates. No release claim, no Deep-as-default change, no commit.)
- Scope: Mechanical R75 correction only, frontend-only. (1) **Pending registration can no longer disable the panel:** the unconditional `await deletionSubscriptionRef.current` before conversation creation was REMOVED together with the now-unused `deletionSubscriptionRef` (superseding R74's "bound and time out" prescription — with trust evaluated at data-read time, any wait before initialization is unnecessary, so initialization proceeds immediately instead of after a bounded delay). Conversation creation, input, sending, and rendering now always start. (2) **Reconciliation with R73/R74:** `sanitizeSources(sources, trusted)` takes the caller's trust explicitly; trust is captured at the DATA-READ boundary — a load captures `deletionSubscriptionActiveRef.current` immediately BEFORE `api_chat_get_messages`, and a stream captures `streamSourcesTrusted` once at send start for its whole start/done lifecycle. While the subscription is unconfirmed or failed, every result is applied source-free; a LATE successful registration re-enables sources only for loads/streams whose data is read AFTER it (a fresh safe boundary), never for already-stripped stale results, and never re-attaches sources to previously rendered text. A deletion that commits during a pending window is unobservable, but every result that could carry it is rendered source-free by construction. R73's successful-subscription ordering/filtering, R74's fail-closed rejection behavior and bounded generation-tagged retention (64-cap, eviction suppression, epoch purge), the active-stream abort protocol, server-side scrubbing, source parity, and persistence are unchanged; no bus, no second registry. (3) **Mojibake nit:** the three corrupted comment sequences introduced by earlier PowerShell text rewrites (mojibake of "→" and two of "—") in `ChatPanel/index.tsx` were replaced with plain ASCII via byte-level code-point replacement (`U+00E2 U+2020 U+2019` → "to", `U+00E2 U+20AC U+201D` → "-"); the file is now pure ASCII, BOM-less, LF-only (byte-verified). No broad formatting or encoding rewrite was made.
- Files changed: `frontend/src/components/ChatPanel/index.tsx` (gate removal, `sanitizeSources` trust parameter, load trust capture at read start, per-send `streamSourcesTrusted`, comment fixes), `frontend/tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` (R73's blocking-gate test rewritten as the pending-never-resolves usability regression; the deletion-filter test made deterministic under the trust model by deferring conversation creation), this doc.
- Lifecycle semantics (exact): panel mount issues `listen("chat-meeting-deleted", ...)`; `deletionSubscriptionActiveRef` flips true only on confirmed registration; conversation creation starts immediately regardless. Load: trust = subscription-active state captured BEFORE the rows read; untrusted results apply source-free, trusted results filter against the recorded generation-tagged deletions (with overflow suppression) at application. Send: trust captured at send start and held for that stream's start/done, so a stream begun untrusted stays source-free even if registration lands mid-stream. Failure: active stays false forever → everything source-free, raw error logger-only. Overflow: trusted results are stripped for the remainder of the generation. Scope change: purge + degradation lift as in R74.
- Tests: `keeps conversation, input, and sending usable while the deletion listener never registers` (NEW — registration promise never settles: the conversation invoke RUNS, listener callback never installed, textarea enabled, send completes, done text renders source-free, no raw listener error anywhere); `filters a locally deleted meeting's sources from a load result that resolves after the deletion` (now deterministic: conversation creation deferred, registration confirmed active before the rows read, deletion recorded mid-flight, filtered application). R74's rejection and overflow regressions and all R53-R73 chat regressions remain green unchanged. **Fail proof:** with an unresolved await temporarily re-inserted at the top of `loadConversation` (R74-era blocking), the pending test FAILS (conversation never created); restored, it passes.
- Verification (repo root; `$env:CARGO_TARGET_DIR = "C:\Users\arman\cargo-target"`):
  - `cargo test --manifest-path "frontend/src-tauri/Cargo.toml" --lib` — ✓ 835 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored (no Rust change this round).
  - `cargo check --manifest-path "src-tauri/Cargo.toml"` — ✓ with only the documented pre-existing `Sidebar` dead-code warning.
  - `pnpm run typecheck` — ✓ (`tsc --noEmit` clean).
  - `npx vitest run` — ✓ 112 passed / 20 files (count unchanged: one test rewritten, one added net-zero with R74's two and all prior regressions green); focused `npx vitest run tests/components/chat-scope.test.tsx` — ✓ 32 passed.
  - `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check` on the touched Rust files — ✓ clean (untouched this round, re-verified); `git diff --check` — ✓ (only the pre-existing CRLF→LF notice for `frontend/src/types/index.ts`). The excluded evaluation suite was intentionally NOT run.
- Notes/decisions:
  1. **Removal over bounded wait.** The reviewer's alternative ("bound listener setup and, on timeout, proceed") is strictly weaker than removing the wait: with trust evaluated at data-read time, waiting adds latency without adding safety. The safe boundary a late registration must establish is exactly the fresh read/send trust capture, which is structural.
  2. **Why per-send trust for streams.** A stream started while untrusted could have its stripped start sources re-introduced by a late done event if done evaluated trust at event time; holding the send-start decision for the whole lifecycle closes that window. Deletions committing during a trusted stream are observed through the still-active subscription and scrubbed via the recorded set and the backend abort path.
  3. **Mojibake handling.** The Edit-tool text round-trip could not reproduce the corrupted byte sequences reliably, so the fix used .NET code-point replacement (U+00E2 U+2020 U+2019 → "to", U+00E2 U+20AC U+201D → "-") written BOM-less; post-fix byte scan reports zero non-ASCII characters in the file. No source file was round-tripped through PowerShell text cmdlets after this point.

### Review R78 — corrective final Sprint 4 architecture review after R77
- Date: 2026-09-03

### Task HR-4.5.R78 — R78 remediation: one shared open-admission budget, reserved final-revalidation slice with revalidated-Fast fallback [M]
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Implementer model: z-ai/glm-5.3-flash
- Status: done (implementation with focused tests on branch `sprint-2/durable-local-index`, HEAD `093ff38` worktree; also closes the R78-review R79 follow-up scope, Fast fallback within the total Deep deadline. Task HR-4.5 remains **Blocked** on its four inherited Sprint 3 gates. No release claim, no Deep-as-default change, no commit.)
- Scope: Mechanical R78 correction only, confined to the Deep retrieval agent (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs`) plus its tests. (1) **One shared open-admission budget:** explicit `openMeetingIds` and implicit range-free summary/notes expansions now consume ONE budget - at most `PLANNER_MAX_OPENS_PER_ROUND` (5) distinct meetings opened per planner round and `PLANNER_MAX_OPENS_TOTAL` (8) across the request. Explicit opens keep their existing admission-time enforcement; each range-free expansion is checked at execution immediately BEFORE its `load_meeting_source_head_with_cancellation` call against a per-round counter seeded with the round's admitted explicit opens, so an exhausted budget skips it with zero load/hydration work. Dedupe (already-opened meetings), per-round capability allow-lists (cards/expandable evidence), the ten-expansion parser cap, and scope fencing are unchanged; no new planner field or client concept. (2) **Reserved final-revalidation slice with revalidated-Fast fallback:** `DeepBounds` gains `final_revalidation_reserve` (production 5s, `FINAL_REVALIDATION_RESERVE`). The planner loop stops early when only the reserve remains; the mandatory final hydration fence normally runs on the budget token while it is live; when the NON-USER total budget expires before or DURING the fence, the fence re-runs within the reserved slice under a child of the PARENT token (user cancellation still aborts through it) over the INITIAL Fast/base evidence - snapshotted (`RankedRetrieval: Clone`) before the loop and swapped in for `ranked`/`hydrated`/semantics on fallback, so ranked evidence, semantics, and sources stay exactly consistent. `BudgetExhausted` is no longer reachable after the initial pass: post-fence expiry checks no longer fail the request, because the published evidence is fully re-validated; only the user's own cancellation aborts (re-checked after the synchronous handoff callback). Initial-pass budget expiry still maps to `BudgetExhausted` (no evidence exists yet - the Fast-equivalent outcome). Chat adapters unchanged (`BudgetExhausted` handling simply becomes the rare initial-pass-only path). All R53-R75 invariants, R70 planner authority bounds, and the shared deadline/child-token/watchdog patterns are preserved; no new client, registry, or timeout mechanism.
- Exact counters: per round, `opens_this_round` seeds with `admitted.open_meeting_ids.len()` (<= 5 admitted) and increments only when a range-free expansion passes `opens_this_round < 5 && opened_meetings.len() < 8 && opened_meetings.insert(..)`; failure of any check `continue`s before the loader. Across the request, `opened_meetings` (insertion = open) is capped at 8 by both admission and execution checks.
- Exact deadline flow: round top: budget-cancelled -> break; `remaining <= final_revalidation_reserve` -> break (reserve held). Planner call: `call_deadline = min(call_timeout, remaining)` on a child of `budget` with its own watchdog; parent-cancel -> `Cancelled`; child deadline/budget expiry -> break with current evidence. Final fence: budget live -> hydrate(`ranked`, budget); budget expired at entry -> hydrate(`initial_ranked`, parent child + reserve watchdog); budget expires mid-fence -> same fallback once; fallback success publishes `initial_ranked` + its hydration (Fast evidence, source parity); fallback failure -> `FinalValidation` (fail-closed); parent cancel at any point -> `Cancelled`.
- Files changed: `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs` (`FINAL_REVALIDATION_RESERVE`, `DeepBounds.final_revalidation_reserve`, early-stop, shared per-round counter, `initial_ranked` snapshot, `run_final_hydration` helper, fallback fence, post-fence checks, `BudgetExhausted` doc), `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs` (shared-budget regression; `per_call_deadline_and_total_budget_fall_back_in_time` total-budget sub-case rewritten from the pinned `BudgetExhausted` outcome to the fallback outcome; `budget_expiry_during_the_answer_handoff_callback_publishes_nothing` rewritten as `budget_expiry_during_the_answer_handoff_still_hands_off_validated_evidence`), this doc.
- Tests (all deterministic, hermetic, no network):

Under the old per-round-free execution this test fails (`additional_rounds == 1`, wrong opened set).
  - `per_call_deadline_and_total_budget_fall_back_in_time` (rewritten total-budget sub-case) - planner round starts inside the envelope and is expired by the watchdog (`planner_round_trips == 1`, `additional_rounds == 0`); the fallback publishes the revalidated Fast/base evidence: zero `deep:` evidence ids, all sources from the base meeting, markdown parity, and the `AnswerGeneration` progress stage IS reached (old code asserted `BudgetExhausted` and no handoff). The per-call-deadline sub-case is unchanged and green.
  - `budget_expiry_during_the_answer_handoff_still_hands_off_validated_evidence` (rewritten) - expiry during the synchronous handoff callback no longer fails the request: validated evidence hands off (all sources from the base meeting); the user-cancellation-during-handoff sub-case still aborts with `Cancelled`.
  - `cancellation_during_planner_call_aborts_without_answer_handoff`, `pre_cancelled_token_aborts_before_any_work`, `zero_budget_fails_closed_before_any_work`, and `initial_failures_map_typed_cancellation_by_variant` remain green unchanged, pinning that user cancellation aborts and initial-pass expiry still fails closed.

- Notes/decisions:
  1. **Enforcement placement.** The shared budget is enforced in the execution loop immediately before each range-free load (zero work for excess) rather than in `admit_round_actions`, because the range-free determination (`expand_neighborhood_targets` empty segment ids) resolves against the round's ranked pool at execution; admission still owns capability/dedupe/total checks and seeds the per-round counter with its admitted explicit opens, so admitted-but-unexecuted opens conservatively consume the round budget.

  3. **Reserve as a bound input.** `final_revalidation_reserve` joins `DeepBounds` (fields are inputs precisely so hermetic tests can exercise real timeout paths); production keeps 5s of the 30s envelope, and the planner early-stop keeps the normal fence inside the envelope - the parent-token slice is the bounded safety valve for mid-round expiry, after which a still-failing fence is a typed `FinalValidation` component failure, never a silent un-revalidated publication.

### Review R79 — after Task HR-4.5.R78
- Date: 2026-09-03

- Findings:
  1. **Blocker — the five-second "reserve" is outside, not inside, the hard 30-second Deep-preparation envelope.** The hard budget token still expires at the full `preparation_budget` (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:488-499`), a planner call may consume all remaining time because its deadline is `min(call_timeout, remaining)` rather than excluding the reserve (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:629-650`), and expiry then creates a fresh child of the user token with a new full reserve for fallback hydration (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:985-1011`, `:1027-1061`). Production can therefore run for roughly 35 seconds before answer generation, violating the unchanged architecture MUST of 30 seconds total (`docs/hybrid-rag/architecture.md:1484-1502`). The synchronous handoff is also now allowed to overrun without any total-budget fence (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:1087-1107`); its regression deliberately sleeps 1.2 seconds under a 400 ms budget and expects success (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:2059-2100`). Use one absolute 30-second deadline: stop/cancel Deep-only planner/additional work at `deadline - reserve`, run final/fallback revalidation only in the actual remaining slice under that same hard deadline, and do not mint time after expiry. Add an assertion tight enough to fail when the configured 200 ms/400 ms total is exceeded. Parent cancellation must remain the distinct abort outcome. Also resolve the literal no-`BudgetExhausted`-failure requirement: initial expiry still maps to a user-visible preparation failure in both adapters (`frontend/src-tauri/src/api/chat.rs:1253-1267`, `:1367-1381`).

Its absence-of-final-`deep:`-evidence assertion (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs:889-914`) also cannot distinguish "loader never started" from "loader ran and evidence was later dropped." The implementation itself places the range-free cap check before the head loader and correctly shares the five/eight distinct-meeting sets (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs:764-855`), while existing capability/scope/dedupe tests remain green; add one round containing both explicit and range-free actions plus an observable loader-call seam/counter so the requested no-work property is falsifiable.

Unit/component tests and excluded corpus-free diagnostics cannot substitute for those artifacts.

### Task HR-4.5.R80 — R80 remediation: absolute 30-second Deep deadline including final revalidation, loader-boundary no-work observation [M]
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Implementer model: z-ai/glm-5.3-flash
- Status: done (implementation with focused tests on branch `sprint-2/durable-local-index`, HEAD `093ff38` worktree. Task HR-4.5 remains **Blocked** on its four inherited Sprint 3 gates. No release claim, no Deep-as-default change, no commit.)
- Scope: Mechanical R80 correction only, confined to the Deep retrieval agent (`frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs`) plus its tests. (1) **Absolute 30-second wall-clock deadline including final revalidation:** the R78 fallback's fresh `parent.child_token()` + new reserve watchdog after expiry - which allowed a worst-case 30s + 5s wall clock - was REMOVED. The fence now ALWAYS runs on the existing budget token, whose watchdog caps it at exactly the 30-second envelope, so final revalidation can never extend the wall clock and no fresh post-expiry token is ever minted. The planner call deadline additionally carves the reserve out (`min(call_timeout, remaining - final_revalidation_reserve)`), so a planner call can never spend the slice. Fence target selection moved BEFORE the fence: once only the reserved slice remains (`remaining <= final_revalidation_reserve`) or the budget expired during a round, the fence re-validates the INITIAL Fast/base evidence (R78's `initial_ranked` snapshot, moved into `ranked` on fallback so ranked evidence, semantics, and hydrated sources stay exactly consistent); otherwise it validates the full accumulated `ranked`. A fence that cannot complete inside the envelope fails closed with the typed `BudgetExhausted` (the envelope is genuinely spent - no un-revalidated evidence is ever published and the deadline is never extended); non-cancellation hydration errors remain `FinalValidation`; user cancellation aborts everywhere (`parent` checks at fence entry, post-fence, and after the synchronous handoff callback). R78's fallback, R70 authority bounds, R53-R75 invariants, and the existing watchdog/child-token patterns are preserved; no new client or registry. (2) **Loader-boundary no-work observation:** the shared open-budget regression now seeds every expansion-target meeting with a UNIQUE transcript head (`t-x-{index}`) and asserts the loader-boundary invariant - a started head load of an existing seeded meeting ALWAYS publishes `deep:t-x-{index}` - so each allowed meeting's `deep:t-x-{index}` presence proves its load ran, each budget-exhausted meeting's `deep:t-x-{index}` absence plus the absence of its unique head text from the hydrated markdown proves the loader never started, using only the existing seeded-pool/ranked-evidence seam (no new trait, wrapper, or hook).
- Files changed: `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent.rs` (fence restructure: budget-token-only fence, reserve-zone initial-evidence selection, planner-call reserve carve-out, envelope-miss `BudgetExhausted` mapping), `frontend/src-tauri/src/retrieval/agent/tests.rs` (total-budget sub-case rewritten to the deadline-unblocking in-envelope fallback with wall-clock assertion; NEW `total_envelope_exhaustion_fails_closed_without_publication`; shared-budget test gains unique head transcripts and loader-boundary assertions), this doc.
- Exact deadline flow (R80): round top: budget-cancelled -> break; `remaining <= final_revalidation_reserve` -> break (reserve held). Planner call: `call_deadline = min(call_timeout, remaining - final_revalidation_reserve)` on a child of `budget`; parent-cancel -> `Cancelled`; child-deadline/budget expiry -> break with current evidence. Fence: if `budget.is_cancelled() || elapsed >= budget - reserve` then `ranked = initial_ranked` (Fast fallback target); fence on the budget token -> success publishes validated evidence (Fast base on fallback, full set otherwise) within the envelope; fence failure with budget expired/elapsed -> `BudgetExhausted` (absolute envelope miss, fail-closed); parent cancel -> `Cancelled`; other errors -> `FinalValidation`. Post-fence and post-handoff checks are parent-cancellation-only.
- Tests (deterministic, hermetic, no network):
  - `per_call_deadline_and_total_budget_fall_back_in_time` (total-budget sub-case rewritten for R80) - `DeadlineUnblockingPlanner` is cut off at `call_deadline = budget - reserve` (125ms of a 200ms budget with a 75ms reserve); the round stops, the reserve-zone fence re-validates the initial Fast/base evidence ON the budget token, and the run completes INSIDE the envelope: `started.elapsed() < 400ms` (no budget+reserve extension, no fresh token), `planner_round_trips == 1`, `additional_rounds == 0`, zero `deep:` evidence ids, all sources from the base meeting, markdown parity, `AnswerGeneration` reached.
  - NEW `total_envelope_exhaustion_fails_closed_without_publication` - a deadline-ignoring planner spends the whole envelope including the reserve: typed `BudgetExhausted`, no `AnswerGeneration` stage, run completed well inside any extension (`< 5s` wall clock, no fresh token).
  - `explicit_and_range_free_opens_share_one_admission_budget` (strengthened) - ten range-free expansions plus three explicit opens across seeded meetings with unique transcript heads: every allowed meeting publishes `deep:t-x-{n}`, every budget-exhausted meeting has neither `deep:` evidence nor its unique head text in the markdown (loader never started), total opened == 8, `additional_rounds == 2`, `planner_round_trips == 2`.
  - All other agent tests (user-cancel abort, zero budget, per-call deadline child cancellation, R70 open/expand caps) and the full lib suite remain green: 837 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored (net +1 for the new exhaustion test).
- Verification (repo root; `$env:CARGO_TARGET_DIR = "C:\Users\arman\cargo-target"`):
  - `cargo test --manifest-path "frontend/src-tauri/Cargo.toml" --lib` - checkmark 837 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored (was 836: net +1).
  - `cargo check --manifest-path "src-tauri/Cargo.toml"` - checkmark with only the documented pre-existing `Sidebar` dead-code warning.
  - `pnpm run typecheck` - checkmark (`tsc --noEmit` clean); `npx vitest run` - checkmark 112 passed / 20 files (frontend untouched this round).
  - `rustfmt --edition 2021 --check` on the touched Rust files - checkmark clean; `git diff --check` - checkmark (only the pre-existing CRLF->LF notice for `frontend/src/types/index.ts`). The excluded evaluation suite was intentionally NOT run.
- Notes/decisions:
  1. **Why no post-expiry token preserves the fallback.** The reserve + planner-deadline carve-out guarantee the fence STARTS with at least the slice remaining in the normal path; the fallback is therefore chosen BEFORE the fence (target = initial Fast/base evidence) and executes on the budget token, which is what makes the 30-second bound absolute. The only remaining expiry failure is the degenerate case where even the smaller Fast revalidation could not finish inside the envelope - a genuinely spent deadline, typed `BudgetExhausted`, never a silent un-revalidated publication.
  2. **Loader-boundary seam.** The loader is a free repository function with no counting hook, so the smallest existing seam is its guaranteed publication signature: a head load of an existing seeded meeting always appends the meeting's unique `deep:t-{segment}` transcript evidence to `ranked`. Absence of that id (and of the head text in the hydrated markdown) for budget-exhausted meetings therefore proves the loader never started, while presence for allowed meetings proves allowed loads still run.
  3. **Error mapping.** Envelope-miss during the fence maps to `BudgetExhausted` (the accurate typed outcome: the 30-second budget was fully spent before validation completed) instead of `FinalValidation`; the chat adapters' existing `BudgetExhausted` message remains accurate. Genuine hydration component errors still map to `FinalValidation`.
- Inherited Sprint 3 release gates (unchanged, each still absent): valid independently authored Portuguese corpus; production-path quality and final provider-answer evidence; native Windows/R13 hermetic session evidence; exact-head GitHub Actions evidence. Deep latency/round-trip/quality metrics and native smoke remain unmeasured/unrun; nothing unrun is reported as passed.

### Task HR-4.5.R82 — R82 remediation: absolute pre-final deadline token for every Deep-only action, direct loader-invocation instrumentation [M]
- Date: 2026-09-03
- Implementer model: z-ai/glm-5.3-flash
- Status: done (implementation with focused tests on branch `sprint-2/durable-local-index`, HEAD `093ff38` worktree. Task HR-4.5 remains **Blocked** on its four inherited Sprint 3 gates. No release claim, no Deep-as-default change, no commit.)
- Scope: Mechanical R82 correction only, confined to the Deep retrieval agent plus its tests. (1) **One absolute pre-final token for every Deep-only action:** a `pre_final` child of `budget` with its own watchdog at `preparation_budget - final_revalidation_reserve` now governs EVERY Deep-only operation - the initial retrieval and initial hydration, per-round planner calls (whose per-call child/watchdog chain from it), additional retrievals, explicit-open head-loads, range-free and neighborhood expansion loads, round scope revalidation (`revalidate_ids_in_scope`), `rank_with_mode`, and round hydration. All in-loop deadline guards (`budget.is_cancelled()`) switched to `pre_final.is_cancelled()`, so Deep-only work stops promptly at the pre-final boundary instead of spending the reserved slice. The planner call deadline keeps the R80 carve-out (`min(call_timeout, remaining - final_revalidation_reserve)`) and its child now chains from `pre_final`, so a call is doubly bounded. `initial_failure` takes the pre-final token: a pre-final boundary miss during the initial pass is typed `BudgetExhausted` (a deadline miss, not a user cancellation). (2) **Final fence and handoff inside the original deadline:** unchanged from R80 - the fence runs on the `budget` token (watchdog at exactly the 30-second envelope, no fresh post-expiry token), reserve-zone target selection picks the initial Fast/base evidence, fence failure inside the envelope is typed `BudgetExhausted` (envelope miss) or `FinalValidation` (component error), and the pre/post-handoff parent-cancellation checks abort with `Cancelled`. (3) **Direct loader-invocation instrumentation:** a `#[cfg(test)]` thread-local `HEAD_LOAD_STARTS` counter (following the existing `#[cfg(test)] request_count` precedent) is incremented at BOTH head-loader invocation sites immediately before the calls; `#[tokio::test]` runs on the current thread, so the counter is exact per test. The shared open-budget regression now resets it, asserts exactly `PLANNER_MAX_OPENS_TOTAL` (8) head-loader starts for thirteen issued open actions (5 round-1 range-free + 3 round-2 explicit), and keeps the R80 loader-boundary publication assertions (`deep:t-x-{n}` present for allowed, absent for exhausted) - so any budget-exhausted invocation would make the count exceed eight and fail. R53-R81 invariants, the R78 shared open-admission budget, R80's absolute fence/no-fresh-token behavior, user-cancellation typing, and source parity are preserved; no new client, registry, or production timing behavior.

### Task HR-4.5.R84 — R84 remediation: full-budget initial Fast base pass, post-base pre-final arming, hard post-fence/post-handoff deadline checks, mixed same-action open test [M]
- Date: 2026-09-03

- Scope: Mechanical R84 correction only, confined to the Deep retrieval agent plus its tests. (1) **Full budget token for the initial Fast base pass:** the initial retrieval and initial hydration run on the full `budget` token (watchdog at exactly the 30-second envelope) - they produce the Fast-equivalent base evidence and are not Deep-only expansion work, so the pre-final boundary must not cut them. (2) **Pre-final arming after the base succeeds:** the `pre_final` watchdog is spawned only AFTER the initial retrieval and hydration succeed, with `after = (preparation_budget - final_revalidation_reserve) - elapsed` (saturating), so its fire time remains the absolute run-start boundary (30s minus the reserve) regardless of how long the base pass took; every subsequent Deep-only action (planner calls, additional retrievals, both head-loads, revalidation, ranking, round hydration) still carries `pre_final`/its children exactly as R82 threaded them, and all in-loop guards test `pre_final`. (3) **Hard deadline checks after final hydration and after the handoff:** restored - after a successful fence and again after the synchronous `AnswerGeneration` handoff callback, `budget.is_cancelled() || elapsed >= preparation_budget` fails the request closed with the typed `BudgetExhausted`: no successful result/publication may leave the function after the absolute 30-second envelope expired, even when the evidence itself was fully validated. The fence still runs on the budget token (absolute cap, no fresh post-expiry token), the reserve-zone target selection still re-validates the initial Fast/base evidence, and user cancellation still aborts as `Cancelled`. (4) **Post-prompt zero-deadline re-check:** the remaining-time/reserve check moved to AFTER `build_planner_prompt` - a planner call can never start with a zero (or reserve-eating) deadline, and `planner_round_trips` is only incremented for calls that actually start. (5) **Same-action mixed open regression:** the shared open-budget test now scripts ONE planner action carrying BOTH five explicit `openMeetingIds` AND ten range-free `expandEvidenceIds` from distinct meetings: the explicit opens consume the entire per-round budget (5), every range-free expansion in the SAME action is skipped before any load work, and round 2's three explicit opens bring the total to exactly `PLANNER_MAX_OPENS_TOTAL` (8) - with the direct `HEAD_LOAD_STARTS` instrumentation asserting exactly eight head-loader invocations for eighteen issued open actions.
